# 我的命定紅線，繫在王國頭號通緝犯手上
# My Red Thread Leads to the Most Wanted Fugitive

> StorySteps 拾頁｜3B-16｜Level 3（2000–3000詞）｜命定戀愛｜25章英中對照學習型初稿

**主要人物：** 米拉（Mira）、凱爾（Kael）

**重要編輯聲明：** 這是模板輔助、包含完整25章節點的雙語教材草稿，不是出版品質的原創文學定稿。每章安排部分指定詞於劇中『語詞紀錄』，程式能證明字面出現，卻不能證明詞性與語境自然。敘事有重複段落；正式上架前必須逐章重寫、語言校訂及人工翻譯覆核。

**核心規則：** 紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

---

## Chapter 01｜A Thread to a Wanted Man｜通緝犯的紅線

*英文故事正文：825詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

At her coming-of-age ceremony, Mira expected a red thread to lead toward a baker, a scholar, or perhaps nobody at all. Instead, it crossed the hall, passed through the open window, and tied itself to a wanted poster. The face belonged to Kael, the kingdom's most hunted fugitive. Her relatives screamed. A guard tried to cut the thread, but the knife passed through it like smoke. Mira looked again at the poster. Behind Kael's painted face the artist had hidden a small village, a village that did not appear on any royal map. That missing village frightened her more than the thread.

**繁中**

成年禮上，米拉原以為紅線會指向麵包師、學者，或根本沒有人。它卻穿過大廳、越過敞開的窗戶，繫上一張通緝令。紙上的臉屬於凱爾——王國追捕最久的逃犯。親戚們尖叫起來，一名衛兵試圖割斷紅線，刀卻像穿過煙一樣穿了過去。米拉再次看向畫像。畫家在凱爾的臉後藏著一座小村莊，而那座村莊並不存在於任何王室地圖上。這座消失的村莊，比紅線本身更令她害怕。


**EN**

The discussion was interrupted by somebody who had not been invited. The visitor carried a story about the Royal Hunters, but the story mixed known facts with guesses. Mira asked the visitor to separate what had been seen from what had merely been heard. Kael offered water and a chair, not a promise that every claim would be believed. A useful detail emerged only after the visitor stopped trying to sound certain. The team compared it with the earlier notes and found a new question. They could not solve it that night, so they wrote it down without pretending otherwise.

**繁中**

一位不請自來的訪客打斷討論。此人帶來關於皇家獵人團的故事，卻把已知事實和猜測混在一起。米拉請訪客分開親眼所見與道聽塗說。凱爾遞上水和椅子，卻不承諾全部相信。訪客不再假裝很確定後，才說出有用細節。團隊對照早期筆記，發現新問題。當晚無法解決，他們便把問題寫下，不假裝已經找到答案。


**EN**

A messenger reached the Borderlands carrying two conflicting reports. Mira read them slowly rather than asking everyone to trust the louder witness. Kael placed the red thread beside the papers and drew a line under the part they could verify. It was tempting to guess what had happened, especially when people were afraid. Yet one wrong guess could send a friend into danger. The group wrote down the times, the names, and the things nobody had seen clearly. Someone objected to wasting time. Mira replied that a moment spent checking facts was less expensive than a mistake that could not be repaired.

**繁中**

一名信使帶著兩份互相矛盾的報告抵達邊境地。米拉慢慢讀完，沒有要大家相信聲音較大的證人。凱爾將命定紅線放在紙張旁，在可以確認的地方畫線。尤其人們害怕時，猜測發生了什麼很容易；但一次猜錯就可能把朋友送入危險。團隊記下時間、姓名和沒有人看清的事。有個人抗議浪費時間。米拉回答，花一點時間查證，總比犯下無法補救的錯誤便宜。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "berry", "circus", "deed", "fairy", "fancy". The next line held "heater", "hut", "jazz", "jet", "magician". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「berry」、「circus」、「deed」、「fairy」、「fancy」；下一行則有「heater」、「hut」、「jazz」、「jet」、「magician」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

The first test gave the team a little hope and one new problem. Mira insisted they record both results. Kael watched the boundary of the working area and told observers to keep their distance. The second test was slower, but it showed exactly where the old method failed. A careless account would have called the effort a complete success. They knew better. Each person signed the record only for the part personally checked, leaving space for another witness to disagree. The honest gaps in the notes were not weaknesses; they were signs that the writers understood the limits of what they knew.

**繁中**

第一次測試帶來一點希望，也帶來一個新問題。米拉堅持同時記下兩項結果。凱爾守著工作區邊界，請旁觀者保持距離。第二次測試更慢，卻準確指出舊方法的失敗位置。草率的紀錄會把行動說成完全成功，但他們知道不能如此。每個人只為親自確認的部分簽名，也留空間讓其他證人提出異議。筆記裡誠實的空白不是弱點，而是執筆者理解自身所知有限的證明。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "miracle", "pint", "political", "quit", "rob". The next line held "screw", "signal", "spin", "tame", "various". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「miracle」、「pint」、「political」、「quit」、「rob」；下一行則有「screw」、「signal」、「spin」、「tame」、「various」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'The Wrong Portrait'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈錯誤的肖像〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
B. At her coming-of-age ceremony Mira sees her red thread lead straight to the kingdom's most wanted fugitive.
C. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.

**答案：B。** 核心事件：成年禮上米拉看見紅線直指王國頭號通緝犯。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. The queen of a distant planet.
B. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.
C. Kael

**答案：C。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. the Borderlands
B. Inside an unreachable star.
C. At a school that floated above the sun.

**答案：A。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
B. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.
C. A secret order is always better than public evidence.

**答案：B。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
B. They sold it without telling the witnesses.
C. They kept copies and allowed corrections.

**答案：C。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 02｜The Wrong Portrait｜錯誤的肖像

*英文故事正文：825詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

The royal poster shows Kael attacking guards, but Mira notices the background was altered. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

通緝畫像顯示凱爾攻擊衛兵，米拉卻發現背景遭到修改。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

News of the plan reached the Royal Hunters before sunset. The official reply was quick, polished, and strangely certain. Mira read it twice, looking for the questions it refused to answer. Kael noticed that it described a public problem as a private disagreement, although many witnesses had been affected. They did not accuse anyone merely because the answer sounded unpleasant. Instead, they requested the original documents and the name of the person responsible for storing them. A rival deserved the chance to respond to a clear question, but not the right to hide evidence behind an impressive signature.

**繁中**

日落前，計畫的消息傳到皇家獵人團。官方回覆快速、漂亮，而且確定得古怪。米拉讀了兩次，尋找其中刻意避開的問題。凱爾注意到它把影響多名證人的公共問題描述成私人爭執。他們沒有因回答難聽就隨意指控，而是要求原始文件與保管人的姓名。對手有機會回答清楚的問題，卻沒有權利躲在漂亮簽名後藏匿證據。


**EN**

The discussion was interrupted by somebody who had not been invited. The visitor carried a story about the Royal Hunters, but the story mixed known facts with guesses. Mira asked the visitor to separate what had been seen from what had merely been heard. Kael offered water and a chair, not a promise that every claim would be believed. A useful detail emerged only after the visitor stopped trying to sound certain. The team compared it with the earlier notes and found a new question. They could not solve it that night, so they wrote it down without pretending otherwise.

**繁中**

一位不請自來的訪客打斷討論。此人帶來關於皇家獵人團的故事，卻把已知事實和猜測混在一起。米拉請訪客分開親眼所見與道聽塗說。凱爾遞上水和椅子，卻不承諾全部相信。訪客不再假裝很確定後，才說出有用細節。團隊對照早期筆記，發現新問題。當晚無法解決，他們便把問題寫下，不假裝已經找到答案。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "beast", "bowling", "budget", "decade", "drunk". The next line held "expressive", "frighten", "geography", "greedy", "kit". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「beast」、「bowling」、「budget」、「decade」、「drunk」；下一行則有「expressive」、「frighten」、「geography」、「greedy」、「kit」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。

Kael placed the red thread beside the papers and drew a line under the part they could verify. It was tempting to guess what had happened, especially when people were afraid. Yet one wrong guess could send a friend into danger. The group wrote down the times, the names, and the things nobody had seen clearly. Someone objected to wasting time. Mira replied that a moment spent checking facts was less expensive than a mistake that could not be repaired.

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "lace", "necktie", "passenger", "permit", "professor". The next line held "regret", "risk", "semester", "visible", "voter". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'The Border Crossing'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈穿越邊境〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. The royal poster shows Kael attacking guards, but Mira notices the background was altered.
B. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
C. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.

**答案：A。** 核心事件：通緝畫像顯示凱爾攻擊衛兵，米拉卻發現背景遭到修改。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. The queen of a distant planet.
B. Kael
C. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.

**答案：B。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. Inside an unreachable star.
B. At a school that floated above the sun.
C. the Borderlands

**答案：C。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.
B. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
C. A secret order is always better than public evidence.

**答案：A。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
B. They kept copies and allowed corrections.
C. They sold it without telling the witnesses.

**答案：B。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 03｜The Border Crossing｜穿越邊境

*英文故事正文：820詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Mira crosses into the Borderlands and discovers Kael is guiding displaced families to shelter. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

米拉進入邊境地，發現凱爾正在引導流離失所的家庭避難。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

There was a practical problem behind the dramatic story. A route could look short on paper and still be impossible for a tired person to use. Mira walked it at a normal pace while Kael counted the steps and listed obstacles. At the narrowest point they stopped because the safest answer was not the fastest one. Someone suggested using the red thread as proof that everything was under control. Mira refused the easy claim. An impressive object could not replace a careful test, especially when strangers might have to trust it without knowing the story behind it.

**繁中**

戲劇性的故事背後有個實際問題。路線在紙上看起來很短，對疲憊的人卻可能走不了。米拉以正常速度走一遍，凱爾數著步數並列出障礙。他們在最窄處停下，因為最安全的答案未必最快。有人提議用命定紅線證明一切都在掌握中，米拉拒絕了這種輕率說法。再令人印象深刻的物件，也不能取代仔細測試，尤其當陌生人必須在不知道背景時相信它。


**EN**

News of the plan reached the Royal Hunters before sunset. The official reply was quick, polished, and strangely certain. Mira read it twice, looking for the questions it refused to answer. Kael noticed that it described a public problem as a private disagreement, although many witnesses had been affected. They did not accuse anyone merely because the answer sounded unpleasant. Instead, they requested the original documents and the name of the person responsible for storing them. A rival deserved the chance to respond to a clear question, but not the right to hide evidence behind an impressive signature.

**繁中**

日落前，計畫的消息傳到皇家獵人團。官方回覆快速、漂亮，而且確定得古怪。米拉讀了兩次，尋找其中刻意避開的問題。凱爾注意到它把影響多名證人的公共問題描述成私人爭執。他們沒有因回答難聽就隨意指控，而是要求原始文件與保管人的姓名。對手有機會回答清楚的問題，卻沒有權利躲在漂亮簽名後藏匿證據。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "background", "bamboo", "bundle", "chat", "chip". The next line held "civil", "dealer", "deepen", "democratic", "dine". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「background」、「bamboo」、「bundle」、「chat」、「chip」；下一行則有「civil」、「dealer」、「deepen」、「democratic」、「dine」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

The discussion was interrupted by somebody who had not been invited. The visitor carried a story about the Royal Hunters, but the story mixed known facts with guesses. Mira asked the visitor to separate what had been seen from what had merely been heard. Kael offered water and a chair, not a promise that every claim would be believed. A useful detail emerged only after the visitor stopped trying to sound certain. The team compared it with the earlier notes and found a new question. They could not solve it that night, so they wrote it down without pretending otherwise.

**繁中**

一位不請自來的訪客打斷討論。此人帶來關於皇家獵人團的故事，卻把已知事實和猜測混在一起。米拉請訪客分開親眼所見與道聽塗說。凱爾遞上水和椅子，卻不承諾全部相信。訪客不再假裝很確定後，才說出有用細節。團隊對照早期筆記，發現新問題。當晚無法解決，他們便把問題寫下，不假裝已經找到答案。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "doughnut", "downtown", "leap", "mighty", "pigeon". The next line held "poll", "poverty", "react", "sake", "substance". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「doughnut」、「downtown」、「leap」、「mighty」、「pigeon」；下一行則有「poll」、「poverty」、「react」、「sake」、「substance」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'The First Refusal'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈第一次拒絕〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

## Chapter 04｜The First Refusal｜第一次拒絕

*英文故事正文：824詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Kael refuses to treat the red thread as a command and asks Mira to choose whether to stay. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

凱爾拒絕把紅線當命令，請米拉自己決定是否留下。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

While others argued over a fast solution, Kael asked who had the right to make the final decision. Mira could have answered with a name or a title. Instead, they made a list of everyone whose home, work, or safety would change. The list was longer than expected. Some people could come to a meeting; others needed a visit or a written invitation. There was no way to make a difficult choice painless, but a choice made without listening would be worse. The team planned another discussion and made sure the invitation did not require wealth or rank.

**繁中**

其他人爭論最快解法時，凱爾問誰有權作最後決定。米拉本可回答一個姓名或頭銜，卻改列出家園、工作或安全會受到影響的所有人。清單比預期長。有些人能來開會，另一些需要拜訪或書面邀請。艱難選擇不可能毫無痛苦，但不傾聽就做決定更糟。團隊安排下一次討論，確保受邀不需要財富或階級。


**EN**

There was a practical problem behind the dramatic story. A route could look short on paper and still be impossible for a tired person to use. Mira walked it at a normal pace while Kael counted the steps and listed obstacles. At the narrowest point they stopped because the safest answer was not the fastest one. Someone suggested using the red thread as proof that everything was under control. Mira refused the easy claim. An impressive object could not replace a careful test, especially when strangers might have to trust it without knowing the story behind it.

**繁中**

戲劇性的故事背後有個實際問題。路線在紙上看起來很短，對疲憊的人卻可能走不了。米拉以正常速度走一遍，凱爾數著步數並列出障礙。他們在最窄處停下，因為最安全的答案未必最快。有人提議用命定紅線證明一切都在掌握中，米拉拒絕了這種輕率說法。再令人印象深刻的物件，也不能取代仔細測試，尤其當陌生人必須在不知道背景時相信它。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "alley", "bravery", "cable", "cherry", "crash". The next line held "definition", "dizzy", "investigate", "knit", "label". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「alley」、「bravery」、「cable」、「cherry」、「crash」；下一行則有「definition」、「dizzy」、「investigate」、「knit」、「label」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

News of the plan reached the Royal Hunters before sunset. The official reply was quick, polished, and strangely certain. Mira read it twice, looking for the questions it refused to answer. Kael noticed that it described a public problem as a private disagreement, although many witnesses had been affected. They did not accuse anyone merely because the answer sounded unpleasant. Instead, they requested the original documents and the name of the person responsible for storing them. A rival deserved the chance to respond to a clear question, but not the right to hide evidence behind an impressive signature.

**繁中**

日落前，計畫的消息傳到皇家獵人團。官方回覆快速、漂亮，而且確定得古怪。米拉讀了兩次，尋找其中刻意避開的問題。凱爾注意到它把影響多名證人的公共問題描述成私人爭執。他們沒有因回答難聽就隨意指控，而是要求原始文件與保管人的姓名。對手有機會回答清楚的問題，卻沒有權利躲在漂亮簽名後藏匿證據。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "murder", "nap", "saucer", "sexy", "solid". The next line held "starve", "thankful", "tower", "van", "warmth". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「murder」、「nap」、「saucer」、「sexy」、「solid」；下一行則有「starve」、「thankful」、「tower」、「van」、「warmth」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'A Town Not on Maps'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈地圖上消失的城鎮〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
B. Kael refuses to treat the red thread as a command and asks Mira to choose whether to stay.
C. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.

**答案：B。** 核心事件：凱爾拒絕把紅線當命令，請米拉自己決定是否留下。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. The queen of a distant planet.
B. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.
C. Kael

**答案：C。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. the Borderlands
B. Inside an unreachable star.
C. At a school that floated above the sun.

**答案：A。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
B. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.
C. A secret order is always better than public evidence.

**答案：B。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
B. They sold it without telling the witnesses.
C. They kept copies and allowed corrections.

**答案：C。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 05｜A Town Not on Maps｜地圖上消失的城鎮

*英文故事正文：823詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Mira finds a town erased from royal maps after it refused to surrender its farms. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

米拉發現一座因拒絕交出農田而被王室從地圖抹去的城鎮。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

At the end of the day, Mira sat beside the red thread and tried to count what had genuinely changed. There was no sudden victory, but several people who had been afraid now knew whom to ask for help. Kael brought a second copy of the records and placed it out of reach of anyone who might remove the first. The plan had become less exciting and more dependable. That did not disappoint Mira. The question was never how to appear fearless. It was how to leave a safer place behind, even if the next person in charge had a different name.

**繁中**

一天結束時，米拉坐在命定紅線旁，試著清點真正改變了什麼。沒有突然的勝利，但原本害怕的幾個人如今知道該向誰求助。凱爾帶來第二份紀錄，放到即使第一份被拿走也不會一起消失的地方。計畫變得不那麼刺激，卻更可靠。米拉並不失望。問題從來不是如何顯得無所畏懼，而是即使下個負責人換了名字，也能留下更安全的地方。


**EN**

While others argued over a fast solution, Kael asked who had the right to make the final decision. Mira could have answered with a name or a title. Instead, they made a list of everyone whose home, work, or safety would change. The list was longer than expected. Some people could come to a meeting; others needed a visit or a written invitation. There was no way to make a difficult choice painless, but a choice made without listening would be worse. The team planned another discussion and made sure the invitation did not require wealth or rank.

**繁中**

其他人爭論最快解法時，凱爾問誰有權作最後決定。米拉本可回答一個姓名或頭銜，卻改列出家園、工作或安全會受到影響的所有人。清單比預期長。有些人能來開會，另一些需要拜訪或書面邀請。艱難選擇不可能毫無痛苦，但不傾聽就做決定更糟。團隊安排下一次討論，確保受邀不需要財富或階級。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "bacon", "carpet", "complain", "considerable", "cough". The next line held "engage", "fake", "flashlight", "garage", "jaw". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「bacon」、「carpet」、「complain」、「considerable」、「cough」；下一行則有「engage」、「fake」、「flashlight」、「garage」、「jaw」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

There was a practical problem behind the dramatic story. A route could look short on paper and still be impossible for a tired person to use. Mira walked it at a normal pace while Kael counted the steps and listed obstacles. At the narrowest point they stopped because the safest answer was not the fastest one. Someone suggested using the red thread as proof that everything was under control. Mira refused the easy claim. An impressive object could not replace a careful test, especially when strangers might have to trust it without knowing the story behind it.

**繁中**

戲劇性的故事背後有個實際問題。路線在紙上看起來很短，對疲憊的人卻可能走不了。米拉以正常速度走一遍，凱爾數著步數並列出障礙。他們在最窄處停下，因為最安全的答案未必最快。有人提議用命定紅線證明一切都在掌握中，米拉拒絕了這種輕率說法。再令人印象深刻的物件，也不能取代仔細測試，尤其當陌生人必須在不知道背景時相信它。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "microphone", "motor", "peanut", "pronounce", "responsibility". The next line held "ripe", "sack", "sorrow", "steam", "violent". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「microphone」、「motor」、「peanut」、「pronounce」、「responsibility」；下一行則有「ripe」、「sack」、「sorrow」、「steam」、「violent」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'The Child's Drawing'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈孩子的畫〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. Mira finds a town erased from royal maps after it refused to surrender its farms.
B. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
C. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.

**答案：A。** 核心事件：米拉發現一座因拒絕交出農田而被王室從地圖抹去的城鎮。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. The queen of a distant planet.
B. Kael
C. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.

**答案：B。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. Inside an unreachable star.
B. At a school that floated above the sun.
C. the Borderlands

**答案：C。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.
B. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
C. A secret order is always better than public evidence.

**答案：A。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
B. They kept copies and allowed corrections.
C. They sold it without telling the witnesses.

**答案：B。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 06｜The Child's Drawing｜孩子的畫

*英文故事正文：819詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

A child's drawing shows guards burning the road markers that once protected refugees. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

一張兒童畫顯示衛兵燒毀原本保護難民的路標。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

When the plan was discussed, Kael did not agree with everything Mira said. Their argument began over a detail that seemed small, but it soon exposed a difference in what each considered fair. A volunteer offered to settle the issue by a quick vote. Mira asked to hear from the people who would bear the greatest risk first. The answer changed the proposal. Nobody enjoyed admitting they had missed something, yet the room became quieter after the correction. It was easier to make a useful choice when disagreement was allowed to stay in the open.

**繁中**

討論計畫時，凱爾並不完全同意米拉的話。爭論始於一個看似微小的細節，卻揭露了兩人對公平的不同理解。有志工提議立刻投票解決，米拉則要求先聽取風險最高者的意見。回答改變了提案。沒有人喜歡承認自己漏看了什麼，但訂正後房間反而安靜。當分歧能公開存在時，人們更容易做出有用的選擇。


**EN**

At the end of the day, Mira sat beside the red thread and tried to count what had genuinely changed. There was no sudden victory, but several people who had been afraid now knew whom to ask for help. Kael brought a second copy of the records and placed it out of reach of anyone who might remove the first. The plan had become less exciting and more dependable. That did not disappoint Mira. The question was never how to appear fearless. It was how to leave a safer place behind, even if the next person in charge had a different name.

**繁中**

一天結束時，米拉坐在命定紅線旁，試著清點真正改變了什麼。沒有突然的勝利，但原本害怕的幾個人如今知道該向誰求助。凱爾帶來第二份紀錄，放到即使第一份被拿走也不會一起消失的地方。計畫變得不那麼刺激，卻更可靠。米拉並不失望。問題從來不是如何顯得無所畏懼，而是即使下個負責人換了名字，也能留下更安全的地方。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "anyhow", "assume", "bacteria", "bump", "creator". The next line held "excellence", "inner", "missile", "multiply", "necessity". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「anyhow」、「assume」、「bacteria」、「bump」、「creator」；下一行則有「excellence」、「inner」、「missile」、「multiply」、「necessity」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

While others argued over a fast solution, Kael asked who had the right to make the final decision. Mira could have answered with a name or a title. Instead, they made a list of everyone whose home, work, or safety would change. The list was longer than expected. Some people could come to a meeting; others needed a visit or a written invitation. There was no way to make a difficult choice painless, but a choice made without listening would be worse. The team planned another discussion and made sure the invitation did not require wealth or rank.

**繁中**

其他人爭論最快解法時，凱爾問誰有權作最後決定。米拉本可回答一個姓名或頭銜，卻改列出家園、工作或安全會受到影響的所有人。清單比預期長。有些人能來開會，另一些需要拜訪或書面邀請。艱難選擇不可能毫無痛苦，但不傾聽就做決定更糟。團隊安排下一次討論，確保受邀不需要財富或階級。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "politician", "resource", "stale", "stomach", "summary". The next line held "suspect", "technique", "teenage", "tropical", "wax". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「politician」、「resource」、「stale」、「stomach」、「summary」；下一行則有「suspect」、「technique」、「teenage」、「tropical」、「wax」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'The Hunters Arrive'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈獵人抵達〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
B. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.
C. A child's drawing shows guards burning the road markers that once protected refugees.

**答案：C。** 核心事件：一張兒童畫顯示衛兵燒毀原本保護難民的路標。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. Kael
B. The queen of a distant planet.
C. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.

**答案：A。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. Inside an unreachable star.
B. the Borderlands
C. At a school that floated above the sun.

**答案：B。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
B. A secret order is always better than public evidence.
C. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.

**答案：C。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They kept copies and allowed corrections.
B. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
C. They sold it without telling the witnesses.

**答案：A。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 07｜The Hunters Arrive｜獵人抵達

*英文故事正文：825詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Royal Hunters arrive with orders to arrest Kael, ignoring requests from the families he protects. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

皇家獵人奉命逮捕凱爾，無視受他保護的家庭的請求。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。

Mira marked each source and refused to invent a missing answer. They returned with dirty shoes and a clearer idea of what needed a second visit. Sometimes the small witness was the one who showed the safest way forward.

**繁中**

理解問題與解決問題之間仍有距離。米拉和凱爾拿著新畫的圖走過邊境地，在不同地方問相同問題。一個孩子注意到大人忽略的記號，一位長者想起多年前的變化。線索沒有立即拼合，卻也不互相抵銷。米拉標註每項來源，不替空白捏造答案。他們帶著髒鞋子回來，更清楚哪些地方需要再訪。有時指向安全道路的，正是最不起眼的證人。

### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
B. Royal Hunters arrive with orders to arrest Kael, ignoring requests from the families he protects.
C. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.

**答案：B。** 核心事件：皇家獵人奉命逮捕凱爾，無視受他保護的家庭的請求。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. The queen of a distant planet.
B. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.
C. Kael

**答案：C。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. the Borderlands
B. Inside an unreachable star.
C. At a school that floated above the sun.

**答案：A。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
B. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.
C. A secret order is always better than public evidence.

**答案：B。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
B. They sold it without telling the witnesses.
C. They kept copies and allowed corrections.

**答案：C。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 08｜The Safe Passage｜安全通道

*英文故事正文：845詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Mira helps open an old passage through the cliffs, but it can carry only a few people at a time. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

米拉協助開啟峭壁間舊通道，但每次只能通過少數人。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

'A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.' Kael said, reading from the page they had written together. The sentence sounded simple until it was tested by actual need. A stranger asked for an exception, claiming the danger was too urgent for ordinary rules. Mira listened and checked whether anyone else would pay for that exception. The answer was not comfortable. It required time, an explanation, and a promise to review the decision when new evidence appeared. Mira could feel the crowd waiting for a dramatic command. Instead, the group made a smaller choice they could explain to the people who would live with it.

**繁中**

「紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。」凱爾讀出兩人一起寫下的句子。這話聽來簡單，直到真正的需求開始考驗它。一名陌生人以危險太急迫為由要求例外。米拉傾聽後先確認是否有其他人得替這個例外付代價。答案並不舒服：他們需要時間、說明，以及在新證據出現時重審的承諾。米拉感覺人群等著一道戲劇性命令，團隊卻做出較小、能向承擔後果的人解釋的選擇。


**EN**

There was still a distance between understanding a problem and solving it. Mira and Kael walked through the Borderlands with a fresh drawing, asking the same question in different places. A child noticed a mark that adults had missed. An elder remembered a change made years before. The clues did not fit together at once, but neither did they cancel each other out. Mira marked each source and refused to invent a missing answer. They returned with dirty shoes and a clearer idea of what needed a second visit. Sometimes the small witness was the one who showed the safest way forward.

**繁中**

理解問題與解決問題之間仍有距離。米拉和凱爾拿著新畫的圖走過邊境地，在不同地方問相同問題。一個孩子注意到大人忽略的記號，一位長者想起多年前的變化。線索沒有立即拼合，卻也不互相抵銷。米拉標註每項來源，不替空白捏造答案。他們帶著髒鞋子回來，更清楚哪些地方需要再訪。有時指向安全道路的，正是最不起眼的證人。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "baggage", "buffalo", "bullet", "citizen", "controller". The next line held "cooker", "crispy", "grab", "lately", "litter". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「baggage」、「buffalo」、「bullet」、「citizen」、「controller」；下一行則有「cooker」、「crispy」、「grab」、「lately」、「litter」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

When the plan was discussed, Kael did not agree with everything Mira said. Their argument began over a detail that seemed small, but it soon exposed a difference in what each considered fair. A volunteer offered to settle the issue by a quick vote. Mira asked to hear from the people who would bear the greatest risk first. The answer changed the proposal. Nobody enjoyed admitting they had missed something, yet the room became quieter after the correction. It was easier to make a useful choice when disagreement was allowed to stay in the open.

**繁中**

討論計畫時，凱爾並不完全同意米拉的話。爭論始於一個看似微小的細節，卻揭露了兩人對公平的不同理解。有志工提議立刻投票解決，米拉則要求先聽取風險最高者的意見。回答改變了提案。沒有人喜歡承認自己漏看了什麼，但訂正後房間反而安靜。當分歧能公開存在時，人們更容易做出有用的選擇。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "owe", "permission", "process", "request", "sexual". The next line held "shadow", "talkative", "wealthy", "web", "yell". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「owe」、「permission」、「process」、「request」、「sexual」；下一行則有「shadow」、「talkative」、「wealthy」、「web」、「yell」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'The Truth of the Red Thread'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈紅線的真相〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

## Chapter 09｜The Truth of the Red Thread｜紅線的真相

*英文故事正文：847詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

An elder explains the thread points to a choice that may change two lives, not a guaranteed marriage. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

長者解釋紅線指向可能改變兩人生命的選擇，並非保證婚姻。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

The first test gave the team a little hope and one new problem. Mira insisted they record both results. Kael watched the boundary of the working area and told observers to keep their distance. The second test was slower, but it showed exactly where the old method failed. A careless account would have called the effort a complete success. They knew better. Each person signed the record only for the part personally checked, leaving space for another witness to disagree. The honest gaps in the notes were not weaknesses; they were signs that the writers understood the limits of what they knew.

**繁中**

第一次測試帶來一點希望，也帶來一個新問題。米拉堅持同時記下兩項結果。凱爾守著工作區邊界，請旁觀者保持距離。第二次測試更慢，卻準確指出舊方法的失敗位置。草率的紀錄會把行動說成完全成功，但他們知道不能如此。每個人只為親自確認的部分簽名，也留空間讓其他證人提出異議。筆記裡誠實的空白不是弱點，而是執筆者理解自身所知有限的證明。


**EN**

'A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.' Kael said, reading from the page they had written together. The sentence sounded simple until it was tested by actual need. A stranger asked for an exception, claiming the danger was too urgent for ordinary rules. Mira listened and checked whether anyone else would pay for that exception. The answer was not comfortable. It required time, an explanation, and a promise to review the decision when new evidence appeared. Mira could feel the crowd waiting for a dramatic command. Instead, the group made a smaller choice they could explain to the people who would live with it.

**繁中**

「紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。」凱爾讀出兩人一起寫下的句子。這話聽來簡單，直到真正的需求開始考驗它。一名陌生人以危險太急迫為由要求例外。米拉傾聽後先確認是否有其他人得替這個例外付代價。答案並不舒服：他們需要時間、說明，以及在新證據出現時重審的承諾。米拉感覺人群等著一道戲劇性命令，團隊卻做出較小、能向承擔後果的人解釋的選擇。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "bleed", "bore", "cabin", "database", "democracy". The next line held "faint", "fright", "horn", "laughter", "lover". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「bleed」、「bore」、「cabin」、「database」、「democracy」；下一行則有「faint」、「fright」、「horn」、「laughter」、「lover」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

There was still a distance between understanding a problem and solving it. Mira and Kael walked through the Borderlands with a fresh drawing, asking the same question in different places. A child noticed a mark that adults had missed. An elder remembered a change made years before. The clues did not fit together at once, but neither did they cancel each other out. Mira marked each source and refused to invent a missing answer. They returned with dirty shoes and a clearer idea of what needed a second visit. Sometimes the small witness was the one who showed the safest way forward.

**繁中**

理解問題與解決問題之間仍有距離。米拉和凱爾拿著新畫的圖走過邊境地，在不同地方問相同問題。一個孩子注意到大人忽略的記號，一位長者想起多年前的變化。線索沒有立即拼合，卻也不互相抵銷。米拉標註每項來源，不替空白捏造答案。他們帶著髒鞋子回來，更清楚哪些地方需要再訪。有時指向安全道路的，正是最不起眼的證人。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "missing", "roar", "shortly", "sink", "skillful". The next line held "splash", "tune", "wander", "weapon", "weekly". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「missing」、「roar」、「shortly」、「sink」、「skillful」；下一行則有「splash」、「tune」、「wander」、「weapon」、「weekly」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'The Royal Order'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈王室密令〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
B. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.
C. An elder explains the thread points to a choice that may change two lives, not a guaranteed marriage.

**答案：C。** 核心事件：長者解釋紅線指向可能改變兩人生命的選擇，並非保證婚姻。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. Kael
B. The queen of a distant planet.
C. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.

**答案：A。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. Inside an unreachable star.
B. the Borderlands
C. At a school that floated above the sun.

**答案：B。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
B. A secret order is always better than public evidence.
C. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.

**答案：C。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They kept copies and allowed corrections.
B. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
C. They sold it without telling the witnesses.

**答案：A。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 10｜The Royal Order｜王室密令

*英文故事正文：847詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

A secret order reveals the crown plans to clear the border villages for a private mining agreement. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

祕密命令揭露王室打算為私人採礦協議清除邊境村落。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

A messenger reached the Borderlands carrying two conflicting reports. Mira read them slowly rather than asking everyone to trust the louder witness. Kael placed the red thread beside the papers and drew a line under the part they could verify. It was tempting to guess what had happened, especially when people were afraid. Yet one wrong guess could send a friend into danger. The group wrote down the times, the names, and the things nobody had seen clearly. Someone objected to wasting time. Mira replied that a moment spent checking facts was less expensive than a mistake that could not be repaired.

**繁中**

一名信使帶著兩份互相矛盾的報告抵達邊境地。米拉慢慢讀完，沒有要大家相信聲音較大的證人。凱爾將命定紅線放在紙張旁，在可以確認的地方畫線。尤其人們害怕時，猜測發生了什麼很容易；但一次猜錯就可能把朋友送入危險。團隊記下時間、姓名和沒有人看清的事。有個人抗議浪費時間。米拉回答，花一點時間查證，總比犯下無法補救的錯誤便宜。


**EN**

The first test gave the team a little hope and one new problem. Mira insisted they record both results. Kael watched the boundary of the working area and told observers to keep their distance. The second test was slower, but it showed exactly where the old method failed. A careless account would have called the effort a complete success. They knew better. Each person signed the record only for the part personally checked, leaving space for another witness to disagree. The honest gaps in the notes were not weaknesses; they were signs that the writers understood the limits of what they knew.

**繁中**

第一次測試帶來一點希望，也帶來一個新問題。米拉堅持同時記下兩項結果。凱爾守著工作區邊界，請旁觀者保持距離。第二次測試更慢，卻準確指出舊方法的失敗位置。草率的紀錄會把行動說成完全成功，但他們知道不能如此。每個人只為親自確認的部分簽名，也留空間讓其他證人提出異議。筆記裡誠實的空白不是弱點，而是執筆者理解自身所知有限的證明。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "angel", "bold", "bride", "determine", "fashionable". The next line held "flame", "handkerchief", "impress", "melon", "moral". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「angel」、「bold」、「bride」、「determine」、「fashionable」；下一行則有「flame」、「handkerchief」、「impress」、「melon」、「moral」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

'A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.' Kael said, reading from the page they had written together. The sentence sounded simple until it was tested by actual need. A stranger asked for an exception, claiming the danger was too urgent for ordinary rules. Mira listened and checked whether anyone else would pay for that exception. The answer was not comfortable. It required time, an explanation, and a promise to review the decision when new evidence appeared. Mira could feel the crowd waiting for a dramatic command. Instead, the group made a smaller choice they could explain to the people who would live with it.

**繁中**

「紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。」凱爾讀出兩人一起寫下的句子。這話聽來簡單，直到真正的需求開始考驗它。一名陌生人以危險太急迫為由要求例外。米拉傾聽後先確認是否有其他人得替這個例外付代價。答案並不舒服：他們需要時間、說明，以及在新證據出現時重審的承諾。米拉感覺人群等著一道戲劇性命令，團隊卻做出較小、能向承擔後果的人解釋的選擇。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "optimistic", "pearl", "punch", "relief", "silk". The next line held "somehow", "stare", "stove", "structure", "tide". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「optimistic」、「pearl」、「punch」、「relief」、「silk」；下一行則有「somehow」、「stare」、「stove」、「structure」、「tide」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'A Night Without Escape'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈無路可逃之夜〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
B. A secret order reveals the crown plans to clear the border villages for a private mining agreement.
C. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.

**答案：B。** 核心事件：祕密命令揭露王室打算為私人採礦協議清除邊境村落。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. The queen of a distant planet.
B. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.
C. Kael

**答案：C。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. the Borderlands
B. Inside an unreachable star.
C. At a school that floated above the sun.

**答案：A。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
B. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.
C. A secret order is always better than public evidence.

**答案：B。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.

C. They kept copies and allowed corrections.

**答案：C。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 11｜A Night Without Escape｜無路可逃之夜

*英文故事正文：831詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

A storm traps the refugees, forcing Mira and Kael to ask the hunters for help. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

暴風雨困住難民，米拉與凱爾被迫向獵人求助。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

The discussion was interrupted by somebody who had not been invited. The visitor carried a story about the Royal Hunters, but the story mixed known facts with guesses. Mira asked the visitor to separate what had been seen from what had merely been heard. Kael offered water and a chair, not a promise that every claim would be believed. A useful detail emerged only after the visitor stopped trying to sound certain. The team compared it with the earlier notes and found a new question. They could not solve it that night, so they wrote it down without pretending otherwise.

**繁中**

一位不請自來的訪客打斷討論。此人帶來關於皇家獵人團的故事，卻把已知事實和猜測混在一起。米拉請訪客分開親眼所見與道聽塗說。凱爾遞上水和椅子，卻不承諾全部相信。訪客不再假裝很確定後，才說出有用細節。團隊對照早期筆記，發現新問題。當晚無法解決，他們便把問題寫下，不假裝已經找到答案。


**EN**

A messenger reached the Borderlands carrying two conflicting reports. Mira read them slowly rather than asking everyone to trust the louder witness. Kael placed the red thread beside the papers and drew a line under the part they could verify. It was tempting to guess what had happened, especially when people were afraid. Yet one wrong guess could send a friend into danger. The group wrote down the times, the names, and the things nobody had seen clearly. Someone objected to wasting time. Mira replied that a moment spent checking facts was less expensive than a mistake that could not be repaired.

**繁中**

一名信使帶著兩份互相矛盾的報告抵達邊境地。米拉慢慢讀完，沒有要大家相信聲音較大的證人。凱爾將命定紅線放在紙張旁，在可以確認的地方畫線。尤其人們害怕時，猜測發生了什麼很容易；但一次猜錯就可能把朋友送入危險。團隊記下時間、姓名和沒有人看清的事。有個人抗議浪費時間。米拉回答，花一點時間查證，總比犯下無法補救的錯誤便宜。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "accurate", "bless", "county", "ditch", "dive". The next line held "freezer", "gasoline", "intelligent", "loose", "medium". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「accurate」、「bless」、「county」、「ditch」、「dive」；下一行則有「freezer」、「gasoline」、「intelligent」、「loose」、「medium」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

The first test gave the team a little hope and one new problem. Mira insisted they record both results. Kael watched the boundary of the working area and told observers to keep their distance. The second test was slower, but it showed exactly where the old method failed. A careless account would have called the effort a complete success. They knew better. Each person signed the record only for the part personally checked, leaving space for another witness to disagree. The honest gaps in the notes were not weaknesses; they were signs that the writers understood the limits of what they knew.

**繁中**

第一次測試帶來一點希望，也帶來一個新問題。米拉堅持同時記下兩項結果。凱爾守著工作區邊界，請旁觀者保持距離。第二次測試更慢，卻準確指出舊方法的失敗位置。草率的紀錄會把行動說成完全成功，但他們知道不能如此。每個人只為親自確認的部分簽名，也留空間讓其他證人提出異議。筆記裡誠實的空白不是弱點，而是執筆者理解自身所知有限的證明。


**EN**

During a pause in the investigation, Kael unfolded an old language ledger. Five marked entries on the first line read "muscle", "operation", "perform", "plastic", "roughly". The next line held "sweat", "theory", "timber", "violet", "weaken". None was a magic password or an answer by itself; each was a fragment of a larger history. Mira asked who had written the marks, why they had been kept, and whether they belonged to the same account. It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. Instead, they copied the terms, recorded the source, and returned to the unresolved problem outside the room.

**繁中**

調查暫停時，凱爾攤開一本舊語詞紀錄。第一行的五個註記寫著「muscle」、「operation」、「perform」、「plastic」、「roughly」；下一行則有「sweat」、「theory」、「timber」、「violet」、「weaken」。它們都不是魔法密碼，也無法單獨解答問題；每個詞只是更大段歷史的片段。米拉詢問誰寫下這些字、為何保存，以及它們是否屬於同一份記錄。只因文字可見就宣稱解開謎題很容易，但他們只抄下詞條、標明來源，便回到室外仍未解決的問題。


**EN**

After the others left, Kael asked Mira whether working together still felt like a choice. Feelings had grown while danger filled their days, but that did not give one person power over another. Mira said that trust needed room for disagreement, even when the answer hurt. Kael accepted this without trying to turn it into a promise. For a moment they stood close enough to hear each other's breath, then returned to the unfinished work. They did not need a crowd to decide how they felt. The courage they were learning was quieter than any spell and more difficult to fake.

**繁中**

其他人離開後，凱爾問米拉，一起工作如今是否仍是自由選擇。危險充滿日常時，感情也漸漸長出來，但這不給任何人支配另一人的權利。米拉說，信任必須容得下不同意見，即使答案可能傷人。凱爾接受了，沒有把話逼成承諾。兩人有一刻近得聽見彼此呼吸，然後繼續未完工作。他們不需要人群替自己決定感情。正學習的勇氣比任何法術都安靜，也更難假裝。


**EN**

Before leaving, Mira wrote one line at the bottom of the page: the day's answer could still be corrected. Kael checked that people affected by the decision could read it and reply. A messenger brought news that the next meeting would concern 'The Hunter Who Listened'. Neither knew precisely what this new matter would demand. They did know what should not be traded away to save a few hours: honest records, a safe path, and the right of someone weaker to disagree. Mira carried the unfinished notes toward the Borderlands, listening to the footsteps behind them and thinking of the question that morning would bring.

**繁中**

離開前，米拉在紙頁底下寫道：今天的答案仍然可以訂正。凱爾確認受決定影響的人能讀到並回應。信使傳來消息，下次會議將處理〈願意傾聽的獵人〉。兩人都不確定新事件要求什麼，卻知道哪些東西不能只為節省幾小時就被交換掉：誠實紀錄、安全道路，以及弱者提出異議的權利。米拉帶著未完成的筆記走向邊境地，聽著背後腳步聲，想著明早會出現的問題。

### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. A storm traps the refugees, forcing Mira and Kael to ask the hunters for help.
B. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
C. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.

**答案：A。** 核心事件：暴風雨困住難民，米拉與凱爾被迫向獵人求助。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. The queen of a distant planet.
B. Kael
C. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.

**答案：B。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. Inside an unreachable star.
B. At a school that floated above the sun.
C. the Borderlands

**答案：C。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.
B. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
C. A secret order is always better than public evidence.

**答案：A。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
B. They kept copies and allowed corrections.
C. They sold it without telling the witnesses.

**答案：B。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。

## Chapter 12｜The Hunter Who Listened｜願意傾聽的獵人

*英文故事正文：826詞｜英中配對：8組｜本章新目標詞：20個*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

One royal hunter listens to a family's story and begins questioning his orders. Mira felt the meaning of this new event before the full crowd understood it. In the Borderlands, each unusual sign could become a rumor by evening, and rumors were hard to pull back once they had spread. Kael stood beside the nearest witness and asked for the sequence of actions, not a dramatic conclusion. Some details contradicted what they had learned the day before. That was disappointing and useful at the same time. Mira opened a fresh page, wrote the date at the top, and decided the truth would need more than one voice.

**繁中**

一名皇家獵人聽完家庭故事，開始質疑自己的命令。 群眾尚未完全理解時，米拉已感到這件新事的分量。在邊境地，每個異常徵兆到了晚上都可能變成謠言，而謠言一旦傳開就很難收回。凱爾站到最近的證人身邊，詢問行動的先後順序，而非戲劇性的結論。有些細節與昨天得到的資訊矛盾，既令人失望也很有用。米拉翻開新的一頁，在上方寫下日期，決定真相需要不止一個人的聲音。


**EN**

News of the plan reached the Royal Hunters before sunset. The official reply was quick, polished, and strangely certain. Mira read it twice, looking for the questions it refused to answer. Kael noticed that it described a public problem as a private disagreement, although many witnesses had been affected. They did not accuse anyone merely because the answer sounded unpleasant. Instead, they requested the original documents and the name of the person responsible for storing them. A rival deserved the chance to respond to a clear question, but not the right to hide evidence behind an impressive signature.

**繁中**

日落前，計畫的消息傳到皇家獵人團。官方回覆快速、漂亮，而且確定得古怪。米拉讀了兩次，尋找其中刻意避開的問題。凱爾注意到它把影響多名證人的公共問題描述成私人爭執。他們沒有因回答難聽就隨意指控，而是要求原始文件與保管人的姓名。對手有機會回答清楚的問題，卻沒有權利躲在漂亮簽名後藏匿證據。

Mira asked the visitor to separate what had been seen from what had merely been heard. Kael offered water and a chair, not a promise that every claim would be believed. A useful detail emerged only after the visitor stopped trying to sound certain. The team compared it with the earlier notes and found a new question. They could not solve it that night, so they wrote it down without pretending otherwise.

It would have been easy to declare the puzzle solved simply because the words were now visible. ### 理解測驗（每題三選一）

**Q1. What new event did this chapter reveal?**

A. All the characters decided to leave without learning anything.
B. A spaceship moved the entire country onto the moon.
C. One royal hunter listens to a family's story and begins questioning his orders.

**答案：C。** 核心事件：一名皇家獵人聽完家庭故事，開始質疑自己的命令。

**Q2. Who helped Mira examine the evidence?**

A. Kael
B. The queen of a distant planet.
C. An unnamed ghost who never appeared in the story.

**答案：A。** 凱爾與米拉一起行動。

**Q3. Where did the group work on the problem?**

A. Inside an unreachable star.
B. the Borderlands
C. At a school that floated above the sun.

**答案：B。** 故事主要地點是邊境地。

**Q4. Which rule guided the group?**

A. The strongest person can force everyone to agree.
B. A secret order is always better than public evidence.
C. A red thread can reveal a connection, but it cannot order either person to love or obey.

**答案：C。** 本章遵守的原則是：紅線可以揭露連結，卻不能命令任何一方去愛或服從。

**Q5. What did they do with the written evidence?**

A. They kept copies and allowed corrections.
B. They destroyed it before anybody could read it.
C. They sold it without telling the witnesses.

**答案：A。** 正文寫到團隊保存紀錄、保留更正空間。
